📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: Emerging microbes & infections  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-22  |  DOI: 10.1080/22221751.2026.2732697  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 15, 1, 2732697

包膜糖蛋白中的差异糖基化增强了 DC-SIGN 介导的西非埃博拉病毒 Makona 的细胞进入。

Differential glycosylation in the envelope glycoprotein enhances DC-SIGN-mediated cell entry of the West African Ebolavirus Makona.
作者： Juan García-Bernalt Diego, Fátima Lasala, Joanna Luczkowiak, Clara Delaunay, María A. Noriega, Oscar Malvar, Michel Thépaut, Gonzalo Rivas, Franck Fieschi, Ángeles Canales
摘要中文翻译在 2013-2016 年西非 埃博拉病毒 (EBOV) Makona 流行期间,基因组监测 在 暴发 早期的病毒糖蛋白 (GP) 中发现了 A82V 替换。这种 突变 与感染性增加和病毒传播速度加快有关,但这种增强的 宿主 背后的 宿主 因素仍不清楚。使用假型慢病毒系统,我们发现 GP-A82V 可以增加树突状细胞 (DC) 和巨噬细胞(关键的早期和持续靶细胞)中的病毒结合和感染性。这种增强取决于 DC-SIGN(DC 上的一种凝集素受体)的表达。携带 GP-V82 的丝状病毒样颗粒在表达 DC-SIGN 的细胞中表现出加速的感染动力学。我们鉴定了两个 N 连接糖基化位点 Ngly257 和 Ngly563,它们通过同时接合 DC-SIGN 二聚体来增强 GP-V82 的感染性。A82V 取代增加了可溶性 GP 与 DC-SIGN 的结合。核磁共振分析揭示了 GP-V82 中已知 DC-SIGN 配体 Lewis X (CD15) 的富集。用抗 CD15 抗体阻断 Lewis X 可减少假病毒感染,证实其功能作用。这些发现将 DC-SIGN 确定为导致 EBOV Makona 传播 的关键 宿主 因子,并强调病毒包膜糖基化是促进病毒适应和增强人际传播的机制。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
这种 突变 与感染性增加和病毒传播速度加快有关,但这种增强的 宿主 背后的 宿主 因素仍不清楚。
设计与对象
提供的证据没有明确说明研究设计、设置或研究人群。
核心方法
使用假型慢病毒系统,我们发现 GP-A82V 可以增加树突状细胞 (DC) 和巨噬细胞(关键的早期和持续靶细胞)中的病毒结合和感染性。
主要结果
A82V 取代增加了可溶性 GP 与 DC-SIGN 的结合。
解释与创新
核磁共振分析揭示了 GP-V82 中已知 DC-SIGN 配体 Lewis X (CD15) 的富集。
科研与公卫意义
在 2013-2016 年西非 埃博拉病毒 (EBOV) Makona 流行期间,基因组监测 在 暴发 早期的病毒糖蛋白 (GP) 中发现了 A82V 替换。
局限与证据强度
这些发现将 DC-SIGN 确定为导致 EBOV Makona 传播 的关键 宿主 因子,并强调病毒包膜糖基化是促进病毒适应和增强人际传播的机制。

Differential glycosylation in the envelope glycoprotein enhances DC-SIGN-mediated cell entry of the West African Ebolavirus Makona.

Authors: Juan García-Bernalt Diego, Fátima Lasala, Joanna Luczkowiak, Clara Delaunay, María A. Noriega, Oscar Malvar, Michel Thépaut, Gonzalo Rivas, Franck Fieschi, Ángeles Canales
Original abstract (English)
During the 2013-2016 West African Ebola virus (EBOV) Makona epidemic, genomic surveillance identified an A82V substitution in the viral glycoprotein (GP) early in the outbreak. This mutation was associated with increased infectivity and faster viral spread, yet the host factors underlying this enhanced transmission remained unclear. Using a pseudotyped lentiviral system, we show that GP-A82V increases viral binding and infectivity in dendritic cells (DCs) and macrophages, key early and sustained target cells. This enhancement depends on expression of DC-SIGN, a lectin receptor on DCs. Filovirus-like particles carrying GP-V82 displayed accelerated infection kinetics in DC-SIGN-expressing cells. We identified two N-linked glycosylation sites, Ngly257 and Ngly563, that enhanced GP-V82 infectivity through simultaneous engagement of a DC-SIGN dimer. The A82V substitution increased soluble GP binding to DC-SIGN. Nuclear magnetic resonance analysis revealed enrichment of Lewis X (CD15), a known DC-SIGN ligand, in GP-V82. Blocking Lewis X with an anti-CD15 antibody reduced pseudovirus infection, confirming its functional role. These findings identify DC-SIGN as a key host factor contributing to EBOV Makona transmission and highlight viral envelope glycosylation as a mechanism facilitating viral adaptation and enhanced human-to-human spread.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This mutation was associated with increased infectivity and faster viral spread, yet the host factors underlying this enhanced transmission remained unclear.
Design and population
The supplied evidence does not clearly state the study design, setting, or study population.
Core methods
Using a pseudotyped lentiviral system, we show that GP-A82V increases viral binding and infectivity in dendritic cells (DCs) and macrophages, key early and sustained target cells.
Main results
The A82V substitution increased soluble GP binding to DC-SIGN.
Interpretation and novelty
Nuclear magnetic resonance analysis revealed enrichment of Lewis X (CD15), a known DC-SIGN ligand, in GP-V82.
Scientific and public-health significance
During the 2013-2016 West African Ebola virus (EBOV) Makona epidemic, genomic surveillance identified an A82V substitution in the viral glycoprotein (GP) early in the outbreak.
Limitations and evidence strength
These findings identify DC-SIGN as a key host factor contributing to EBOV Makona transmission and highlight viral envelope glycosylation as a mechanism facilitating viral adaptation and enhanced human-to-human spread.

首次绘制体内埃博拉病毒感染事件图谱

Mapping First In Vivo Ebolavirus Infection Events
作者： Michael A. Barry, Jiani Liu, Brady N. Zell, Lin Wang
摘要中文翻译正埃博拉病毒,包括 埃博拉病毒 (EBOV) 和本迪布焦病毒 (BDBV),可在人类中引起散发性 暴发 和毁灭性出血性感染。阻碍丝状病毒研究的一个重大挑战是,由于传染性病毒具有极高的毒力,只能在生物安全 4 级 (BSL4) 设施中进行处理。使用微型基因组和具有转录/复制能力的病毒样颗粒 (trVLP) 的生命周期建模系统是研究这些病毒生物学和探索 BSL2 条件下 抗病毒药物 疗法的强大工具。迄今为止,这些强大的工具仅在体外细胞培养系统中使用。在这项工作中,我们展示了 EBOV trVLP 的体内使用。我们报告了皮肤注射埃博拉病毒模型中首次感染事件的精细特异性。表达单顺反子 EBOV trVLP 的 Cre 重组酶被包装并用于皮内 (ID) 注射 Cre 报告小鼠。通过荧光素酶成像监测活体动物的初始感染事件,然后通过流式细胞术追踪 Cre 激活的 GFP 的细胞特异性表达。通过这种方法,EBOV trVLP 被证明可以直接感染 T 细胞、单核细胞、树突状细胞 (DC),并且皮肤屏障及其引流淋巴结中的皮肤朗格汉斯细胞 (LC) 的首次感染率显着较高。接下来,将 EBOV trVLP 作为体内工具进行测试,通过测试 EBOV 疫苗 在皮肤感染后降低 trVLP 荧光素酶水平的能力来评估 抗病毒药物 疗法。这项工作首次展示了 EBOV trVLP 的体内使用,并为其用作体内 BSL2 模型以更快地开发 抗病毒药物 药物和 疫苗 提供了概念证明。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决研究丝状病毒的挑战,丝状病毒具有高毒力并且需要 BSL4 设施。该研究试图证明 埃博拉病毒 (EBOV) 具有转录/复制能力的病毒样颗粒 (trVLP) 在体内的使用,为 抗病毒药物 疗法的开发提供 BSL2 模型。
设计与对象
该研究利用 Cre 重组酶在 Cre 报告小鼠皮内 (ID) 中表达单顺反子 EBOV trVLP,以监测活体动物的初始感染事件。
核心方法
在 BSL2 条件下采用使用小基因组和 trVLP 的生命周期建模系统来研究 EBOV 生物学并探索 抗病毒药物 疗法。该研究通过荧光素酶成像和流式细胞术监测初始感染事件,以追踪 Cre 激活的 GFP 的细胞特异性表达。
主要结果
研究表明,EBOV trVLP 可直接感染 T 细胞、单核细胞、树突状细胞 (DC),并且皮肤屏障及其引流淋巴结中的皮肤朗格汉斯细胞 (LC) 的首次感染率显着较高。该研究还证明了使用 EBOV trVLP 作为评估 抗病毒药物 疗法的体内工具。
解释与创新
这项工作为使用 EBOV trVLP 作为体内 BSL2 模型来快速开发 抗病毒药物 制剂和 疫苗 提供了概念证明,满足了丝状病毒体内研究的需求。
科研与公卫意义
这项研究通过实现 EBOV 的体内研究,在该领域取得了重大进展,可以加速 抗病毒药物 疗法和 疫苗 的开发。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到小鼠模型使用的限制,该模型可能无法完全代表人类感染动态。由于依赖非人类模型并且需要在人类研究中进一步验证,证据强度中等。

Mapping First In Vivo Ebolavirus Infection Events

Authors: Michael A. Barry, Jiani Liu, Brady N. Zell, Lin Wang
Original abstract (English)
Orthoebolaviruses, including Ebola virus (EBOV) and Bundibugyo virus (BDBV), cause sporadic outbreaks and devastating hemorrhagic infections in humans. A significant challenge that impedes the study of filoviruses is that the infectious virus can only be handled in biosafety level 4 (BSL4) facilities due to their extreme virulence. Life cycle modeling systems that use minigenomes and transcription/replication-competent virus-like particles (trVLPs) are powerful tools for studying these virus’ biology and to explore antiviral therapies under BSL2 conditions. To date, these powerful tools have only been utilized in vitro in cell culture systems. In this work, we demonstrate the use of EBOV trVLPs in vivo. We report the fine specificity of first infection events in a model of EBOV injection in the skin. Cre recombinase expressing monocistronic EBOV trVLPs were packaged and used to inject Cre reporter mice intradermally (ID). Initial infection events were monitored in living animals by luciferase imaging and then by flow cytometry to track cell-specific expression of Cre-activated GFP. By this approach, EBOV trVLPs were shown to directly infect T cells, monocytes, dendritic cells (DCs) with notably high first infections of skin-resident Langerhans cells (LCs) in the skin barrier and its draining lymph node. EBOV trVLPs were next tested as an in vivo tool to assess antiviral therapies by testing the ability of an EBOV vaccine to reduce trVLP luciferase levels after infection in the skin. This work demonstrates first in vivo use of EBOV trVLPs and provides proof of concept for their use as an in vivo BSL2 model to more rapidly develop antiviral agents and vaccines.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the challenge of studying filoviruses, which are highly virulent and require BSL4 facilities. The research sought to demonstrate the use of Ebola virus (EBOV) transcription/replication-competent virus-like particles (trVLPs) in vivo, providing a BSL2 model for antiviral therapy development.
Design and population
The study utilized Cre recombinase expressing monocistronic EBOV trVLPs in Cre reporter mice intradermally (ID) to monitor initial infection events in living animals.
Core methods
Life cycle modeling systems using minigenomes and trVLPs were employed under BSL2 conditions to study EBOV biology and explore antiviral therapies. The study monitored initial infection events through luciferase imaging and flow cytometry to track cell-specific expression of Cre-activated GFP.
Main results
EBOV trVLPs were shown to directly infect T cells, monocytes, dendritic cells (DCs), with notably high first infections of skin-resident Langerhans cells (LCs) in the skin barrier and its draining lymph node. The study also demonstrated the use of EBOV trVLPs as an in vivo tool to assess antiviral therapies.
Interpretation and novelty
The work provides proof of concept for using EBOV trVLPs as an in vivo BSL2 model to rapidly develop antiviral agents and vaccines, addressing the need for in vivo studies of filoviruses.
Scientific and public-health significance
This study offers a significant advancement in the field by enabling in vivo studies of EBOV, which can accelerate the development of antiviral therapies and vaccines.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by the use of a mouse model, which may not fully represent human infection dynamics. The evidence strength is moderate due to the reliance on a non-human model and the need for further validation in human studies.

Journal: Stochastic Environmental Research and Risk Assessment  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-19  |  DOI: 10.1007/s00477-026-03365-1  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 40, 10
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

埃博拉病毒 暴发 与大气候振荡存在远程联系

Ebola-virus outbreaks are teleconnected with macroclimatic oscillations
作者： Raimundo Real, Miguel A. Farfán, Jesús Olivero, Ana L. Márquez, José C. Báez, Julia E. Fa, Robert Nasi
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在确定宏观气候振荡是否与 埃博拉病毒病 (EBOD) 暴发 遥相关。这解决了了解影响 EBOD 的生态因素以改进 监测 和早期预警系统的需要。
设计与对象
该研究使用人类指数病例数据库,测试了 1976 年至 2025 年非洲 26 个宏观气候振荡指数与 EBOD 暴发 的相关性。
核心方法
研究人员编制了 EBOD 暴发 数据库,并使用监督机器学习算法和模糊逻辑来构建结合四个重要宏观气候指数的模型:太平洋年代际振荡、加勒比海表面温度指数、偶极子模态指数和准两年期振荡。
主要结果
发现四个宏观气候指数与 EBOD 暴发 时空相关。该模型的平均灵敏度为 0.82,特异性为 0.89,成功预测了 2019 年至 2025 年 EBOD 暴发 21 次时空事件中的 14 次。
解释与创新
该研究提供的证据表明,特定的大气候指数可以预测 EBOD 暴发,这可用于开发 EBOD 早期预警系统。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为早期预警系统的开发提供信息,以确定新 EBOD 暴发 的风险,从而加强公共卫生准备和应对。
局限与证据强度
该研究对历史数据的依赖和机器学习模型的使用限制了普遍性。这些模型是在特定时间段进行训练和测试的,这可能不能代表未来的 暴发。

Ebola-virus outbreaks are teleconnected with macroclimatic oscillations

Authors: Raimundo Real, Miguel A. Farfán, Jesús Olivero, Ana L. Márquez, José C. Báez, Julia E. Fa, Robert Nasi
Original abstract (English)
Ebola viruses’ ecology is known to be affected by climate, which is globally monitored using macroclimatic oscillation indices. We tested whether any of 26 macroclimatic oscillation indices were teleconnected with Ebola virus disease (EBOD) outbreaks. We compiled an updated database containing geographical locations and dates of the human index cases for all recorded EBOD outbreaks, all of which occurred in Africa, spanning from 1976 to 2025. We used two spatial resolution scales: (1) the whole area previously considered at least of intermediate biogeographical favourability for the Ebola viruses, and (2) separately in the areas of high and intermediate favourability for the viruses; and two temporal resolution scales (annual and quarterly). We split these data into cases for the period 1976–2018 to train our models, and cases for the period 2019–2025 to test the forecasting ability of the models. Four macroclimatic indices, namely the Pacific Decadal Oscillation, the Caribbean Sea Surface Temperature Index, the Dipole Mode Index and the Quasi-Biennial Oscillation, were spatio-temporally correlated with EBOD outbreaks. Using supervised machine learning algorithms and fuzzy logic, we built a fuzzy logic circuit of ensemble models combining these indices. Average sensitivity for the period 1976–2018 was 0,82 and average specificity was 0,89. The fuzzy logic circuit successfully forecasted the spatio-temporal occurrence of EBOD outbreaks from 2019 to 2025, as 14 out of 21 predictions agreed with observation. These macroclimatic indices can be used as the basis for an early warning system to determine the risk of new EBOD outbreaks.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to determine if macroclimatic oscillations are teleconnected with Ebola virus disease (EBOD) outbreaks. This addresses the need to understand the ecological factors influencing EBOD to improve surveillance and early warning systems.
Design and population
The study tested 26 macroclimatic oscillation indices for their correlation with EBOD outbreaks in Africa from 1976 to 2025, using a database of human index cases.
Core methods
The researchers compiled a database of EBOD outbreaks and used supervised machine learning algorithms and fuzzy logic to build models combining four significant macroclimatic indices: the Pacific Decadal Oscillation, the Caribbean Sea Surface Temperature Index, the Dipole Mode Index, and the Quasi-Biennial Oscillation.
Main results
Four macroclimatic indices were found to be spatio-temporally correlated with EBOD outbreaks. The models had an average sensitivity of 0.82 and specificity of 0.89, and successfully forecasted 14 out of 21 spatio-temporal occurrences of EBOD outbreaks from 2019 to 2025.
Interpretation and novelty
The study provides evidence that specific macroclimatic indices can predict EBOD outbreaks, which could be used to develop an early warning system for EBOD.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings can inform the development of early warning systems to determine the risk of new EBOD outbreaks, enhancing public health preparedness and response.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's reliance on historical data and the use of machine learning models limit generalizability. The models were trained and tested on a specific time period, which may not be representative of future outbreaks.
DOI · 来源DOI · 来源
Journal: Antiviral research  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-21  |  DOI: 10.1016/j.antiviral.2026.106537  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 106537

自我佐剂三价 T4 噬菌体纳米颗粒 疫苗 可诱导针对正埃博拉病毒的广泛保护性免疫力。

A self-adjuvant trivalent T4 bacteriophage nanoparticle vaccine induces broad protective immunity against Orthoebolaviruses.
作者： Junhui Zhou, Xinghai Zhang, Xinfeng Li, Baoyue Zhang, Xiaofan Shen, Weiqi Wang, Yanfeng Yao, Mingyue Zhong, Yiyao Wang, Ying Xie
摘要中文翻译正埃博拉病毒,包括扎伊尔病毒、苏丹病毒和本迪布焦病毒,由于其高病死率和复发性 暴发 仍然是主要的公共卫生威胁。尽管获得许可的 疫苗 可以有效预防埃博拉病毒,但针对多种致病性正埃博拉病毒的广泛保护性 疫苗 仍然无法获得。在这里,我们利用 SpyTag/SpyCatcher 缀合系统开发了基于 T4 噬菌体的纳米颗粒 疫苗,该纳米颗粒显示来自 EBOV、SUDV 和 BDBV 的糖蛋白 (GP)。生成单价 疫苗 候选物和三价制剂 (T4-Mix) 以评估其广泛正埃博拉病毒免疫的潜力。BALB/c 小鼠的肌内免疫无需外源佐剂即可引发强大的 GP 特异性体液和细胞 免疫应答。在使用表达 EBOV、SUDV 或 BDBV GP 的具有复制能力的重组水泡性口炎病毒的同源替代攻击模型中,所有单价 疫苗 均针对各自的攻击病毒提供完全保护。此外,优化的三价 T4-Mix 配方提供了针对 rVSV-EBOV、rVSV-SUDV 和 rVSV-BDBV 挑战的全面保护。总的来说,这些研究结果表明,T4 噬菌体既可作为抗原递送载体,又可作为内在免疫刺激支架,突显其作为开发广泛保护性多价正埃博拉病毒 疫苗 的多功能平台的潜力。

设计与对象
正埃博拉病毒,包括扎伊尔病毒、苏丹病毒和本迪布焦病毒,由于其高病死率和复发性 暴发 仍然是主要的公共卫生威胁。

在使用表达 EBOV、SUDV 或 BDBV GP 的具有复制能力的重组水泡性口炎病毒的同源替代攻击模型中,所有单价 疫苗 均针对各自的攻击病毒提供完全保护。

提供的证据没有明确说明直接观察到的主要结果。

此外,优化的三价 T4-Mix 配方提供了针对 rVSV-EBOV、rVSV-SUDV 和 rVSV-BDBV 挑战的全面保护。

BALB/c 小鼠的肌内免疫无需外源佐剂即可引发强大的 GP 特异性体液和细胞 免疫应答。

总的来说,这些研究结果表明,T4 噬菌体既可作为抗原递送载体,又可作为内在免疫刺激支架,突显其作为开发广泛保护性多价正埃博拉病毒 疫苗 的多功能平台的潜力。

A self-adjuvant trivalent T4 bacteriophage nanoparticle vaccine induces broad protective immunity against Orthoebolaviruses.

Authors: Junhui Zhou, Xinghai Zhang, Xinfeng Li, Baoyue Zhang, Xiaofan Shen, Weiqi Wang, Yanfeng Yao, Mingyue Zhong, Yiyao Wang, Ying Xie
Original abstract (English)
Orthoebolaviruses, including Zaire virus, Sudan virus, and Bundibugyo virus, remain major public health threats because of their high case-fatality rates and recurrent outbreaks. Although licensed vaccines provide effective protection against EBOV, broadly protective vaccines against multiple pathogenic orthoebolavirus species remain unavailable. Here, we developed T4 bacteriophage-based nanoparticle vaccines displaying glycoproteins (GPs) from EBOV, SUDV, and BDBV by leveraging the SpyTag/SpyCatcher conjugation system. Monovalent vaccine candidates and a trivalent formulation (T4-Mix) were generated to evaluate their potential for broad orthoebolavirus immunization. Intramuscular immunization of BALB/c mice elicited robust GP-specific humoral and cellular immune responses without the need for exogenous adjuvants. In homologous surrogate challenge models using replication-competent recombinant vesicular stomatitis viruses expressing EBOV, SUDV, or BDBV GP, all monovalent vaccines conferred complete protection against their respective challenge viruses. Furthermore, the optimized trivalent T4-Mix formulation provided complete protection against rVSV-EBOV, rVSV-SUDV, and rVSV-BDBV challenges. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that the T4 bacteriophage functions as both an antigen delivery vehicle and an intrinsic immunostimulatory scaffold, highlighting its potential as a versatile platform for the development of broadly protective multivalent orthoebolavirus vaccines.

Although licensed vaccines provide effective protection against EBOV, broadly protective vaccines against multiple pathogenic orthoebolavirus species remain unavailable.

Core methods

Interpretation and novelty

Limitations and evidence strength

审查应对西非埃博拉危机的国际战略和合作努力:刚果民主共和国和乌干达 2026 年 Bundibugyo 暴发 的经验教训

Examination of International Strategies and Collaborative Efforts in Responding to the Ebola Crisis in West Africa: Lessons from the 2026 Bundibugyo Outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda
作者： Atabo Amodu, Isaac John Umaru, Chakfa Nanmar, Kerenhappuch Isaac Umaru
摘要中文翻译埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 仍然对全球卫生安全构成重大威胁,特别是在卫生系统脆弱、流行病防范能力有限的非洲地区。本研究通过将 2014-2016 年西非危机的经验教训与刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达的 2026 年本迪布焦病毒 暴发 联系起来,批判性地研究了针对埃博拉病毒 暴发 的国际战略和合作应对措施。该研究借鉴最近的报告和学术文献,分析了国际卫生组织、国家和地区当局以及人道主义合作伙伴实施的应对策略,特别关注 监测、治疗、跨境协调和社区参与。研究结果表明,快速资源调动、协调跨境 暴发 以及持续的社区信任建设是遏制 暴发 的核心。然而,重要的限制仍然存在,特别是缺乏获得许可的 疫苗 和 Bundibugyo 菌株的治疗方法,这限制了可用的医疗干预措施。与2014-2016年西非埃博拉危机的比较进一步表明,尽管在生物医学对策、卫生系统能力和公平获取资源方面仍存在巨大差距,但以往流行病的经验教训有助于改善国际协调和准备工作。该研究的结论是,有效控制埃博拉不仅取决于技术和生物医学干预措施,还取决于国际机构、政府、人道主义行动者和受影响社区之间的持续合作。该研究通过证明将先前 暴发 的经验教训纳入当代应对策略的重要性,为全球卫生安全和流行病防备讨论做出了贡献。其影响强调对研究和创新的持续投资、加强卫生系统、有效的跨境合作、以社区为中心的应对机制以及公平的资源分配,以提高对未来埃博拉暴发的准备。

埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 仍然对全球卫生安全构成重大威胁,特别是在卫生系统脆弱、流行病防范能力有限的非洲地区。

核心方法
该研究借鉴最近的报告和学术文献,分析了国际卫生组织、国家和地区当局以及人道主义合作伙伴实施的应对策略,特别关注 监测、治疗、跨境协调和社区参与。
主要结果
研究结果表明,快速资源调动、协调跨境 暴发 以及持续的社区信任建设是遏制 暴发 的核心。
解释与创新
与2014-2016年西非埃博拉危机的比较进一步表明,尽管在生物医学对策、卫生系统能力和公平获取资源方面仍存在巨大差距,但以往流行病的经验教训有助于改善国际协调和准备工作。
科研与公卫意义
该研究通过证明将先前 暴发 的经验教训纳入当代应对策略的重要性,为全球卫生安全和流行病防备讨论做出了贡献。
局限与证据强度
然而,重要的限制仍然存在,特别是缺乏获得许可的 疫苗 和 Bundibugyo 菌株的治疗方法,这限制了可用的医疗干预措施。

Examination of International Strategies and Collaborative Efforts in Responding to the Ebola Crisis in West Africa: Lessons from the 2026 Bundibugyo Outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda

Authors: Atabo Amodu, Isaac John Umaru, Chakfa Nanmar, Kerenhappuch Isaac Umaru
Original abstract (English)
Ebola virus disease (EVD) remains a major threat to global health security, particularly in African regions characterized by fragile health systems and constrained epidemic preparedness. This study critically examines international strategies and collaborative responses to Ebola outbreaks by relating lessons from the 2014–2016 West African crisis to the 2026 Bundibugyo virus outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. Drawing on recent reports and scholarly literature, the study analyzes response strategies implemented by international health organizations, national and regional authorities, and humanitarian partners, with particular attention to surveillance, treatment, cross-border coordination, and community engagement. The findings indicate that rapid resource mobilization, coordinated cross-border surveillance, and sustained community trust-building were central to outbreak containment. However, important limitations persisted, particularly the absence of licensed vaccines and therapeutics for the Bundibugyo strain, which constrained available medical interventions. Comparison with the 2014–2016 West African Ebola crisis further indicates that lessons from previous epidemics contributed to improvements in international coordination and preparedness, although significant gaps remain in biomedical countermeasures, health-system capacity, and equitable access to resources. The study concludes that effective Ebola control depends not only on technical and biomedical interventions but also on sustained cooperation among international agencies, governments, humanitarian actors, and affected communities. The study contributes to global health security and epidemic preparedness discourse by demonstrating the importance of integrating lessons from previous outbreaks into contemporary response strategies. Its implications emphasize sustained investment in research and innovation, strengthened health systems, effective cross-border collaboration, community-centered response mechanisms, and equitable resource distribution to improve preparedness for future Ebola outbreaks.

Ebola virus disease (EVD) remains a major threat to global health security, particularly in African regions characterized by fragile health systems and constrained epidemic preparedness.

Limitations and evidence strength
However, important limitations persisted, particularly the absence of licensed vaccines and therapeutics for the Bundibugyo strain, which constrained available medical interventions.

刚果民主共和国 2026 年本迪布焦埃博拉 暴发 的动力学、最优控制和 跨物种溢出 风险

Dynamics, Optimal Control, and Spillover Risk of the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola Outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo
作者： Tianmu Chen, Jiahui Li, Shengjie Lai, Yanhua Su, Qiuping Chen, Jia Rui, Zeyu Zhao
摘要中文翻译摘要 2026 年,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 出现本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 (BDBV) 暴发,截至 9 月 6 日,报告确诊病例 6,686 例,死亡 3,226 例,有可能成为全球有记录以来最大的埃博拉病毒 暴发。我们开发了易感者-暴露-感染者-死者康复(SEIDR)模型,结合公众自我保护、安全埋葬、治疗和康复三类干预措施,评估早期传播动态、当前流行轨迹和跨境跨物种溢出风险,并为控制策略的制定提供依据。根据截至 7 月 31 日的累计确诊病例数据,对多个候选开始日期的敏感性分析确定 3 月 28 日为持续 传播 的最佳开始日期,其中 3 月 31 日至 4 月 3 日为最有可能的发病窗口。截至 7 月 31 日,基本再生数 (\(\:{\mathcal{R}}_{0}\)) 为 1.83 (95% CI: 1.81–1.84)。当58.12%的易感人群采取保护行为时,传播链可以被有效中断。通过将非支配排序遗传算法II(NSGA-II)与庞特里亚金最小原理(PMP)相结合,我们得出了时变最优控制策略,每两周调整一次,可以将疫情持续时间缩短约7个月。使用国际移民存量数据和 Facebook 基于 IP 的流动性数据以及 Prophet 预测模型,我们评估了 跨物种溢出 风险。7 月底,有四个国家被确定为极高风险。与现状相比,优化后的控制策略可以大幅降低全球进口风险。非洲刚果民主共和国、欧洲法国和北美加拿大的邻国需要加强入境筛查和准备。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 2026 年本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 (BDBV) 暴发 的 传播 动态和 跨物种溢出 风险,因为它有可能成为有记录以来最大的埃博拉 暴发,截至 2026 年 9 月 6 日报告有 6,686 例确诊病例和 3,226 例死亡。
设计与对象
开发了易感者-暴露-感染者-死者康复(SEIDR)模型来评估早期传播动态、当前流行轨迹和跨境跨物种溢出风险,纳入三类干预措施:公众自我保护、安全埋葬、治疗和康复。
核心方法
该研究利用国际移民存量数据和基于Facebook IP的流动性数据以及Prophet预测模型来评估跨物种溢出风险,并将非支配排序遗传算法II(NSGA-II)与庞特里亚金最小原理(PMP)相结合,得出时变最优控制策略。
主要结果
截至 2026 年 7 月 31 日,基本再生数 (\$\:{\mathcal{R}}_{0}\$) 为 1.83 (95% CI: 1.81–1.84)。敏感性分析确定3月28日是持续传播的最佳开始日期,3月31日至4月3日是最有可能的发病窗口。
解释与创新
作者解释说,当 58.12% 的易感人群采取保护行为时,传播 链可以被有效中断,凸显了社区层面干预措施的潜在影响。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果为制定有针对性的控制策略提供了信息,以减轻本迪布焦埃博拉病毒的传播并降低全球输入风险,特别是在高风险国家。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于截至 2026 年 7 月 31 日的累计确诊病例数据,该数据可能无法反映 暴发 的全部范围。由于依赖基于模型的预测和关于人口行为的假设,证据强度中等。

Dynamics, Optimal Control, and Spillover Risk of the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola Outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo

Authors: Tianmu Chen, Jiahui Li, Shengjie Lai, Yanhua Su, Qiuping Chen, Jia Rui, Zeyu Zhao
Original abstract (English)
Abstract In 2026, a Bundibugyo ebolavirus (BDBV) outbreak emerged in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), with 6,686 confirmed cases and 3,226 deaths reported as of 6 September, potentially becoming the largest Ebola outbreak on record globally. We developed a susceptible-exposed-infectious-deceased-recovered (SEIDR) model incorporating incorporating three categories of interventions, public self-protection, safe burial, and treatment and convalescence, to assess early transmission dynamics, the current epidemic trajectory, and cross-border spillover risk, and to inform the formulation of control strategies. Based on cumulative confirmed case data up to 31 July, sensitivity analyses across multiple candidate start dates identified 28 March as the optimal start date of sustained transmission, with 31 March to 3 April as the most likely onset window. As of 31 July, the basic reproduction number (\(\:{\mathcal{R}}_{0}\)) was 1.83 (95% CI: 1.81–1.84). When 58.12% of the susceptible population adopted protective behaviours, the transmission chain could be effectively interrupted. By integrating the non-dominated sorting genetic algorithm II (NSGA-II) with Pontryagin's minimum principle (PMP), we derived a time-varying optimal control strategy, with adjustments every two weeks, that could shorten the epidemic duration by approximately 7 months. Using International Migrant Stock data and Facebook IP-based mobility data with the Prophet forecasting model, we assessed spillover risk. Four countries were identified as very high risk at the end of July. Compared with the status quo scenario, the optimised control strategy could substantially reduce global importation risk. Enhanced entry screening and preparedness are warranted in neighbouring countries of the DRC in Africa, France in Europe, and Canada in North America.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to assess the transmission dynamics and spillover risk of the 2026 Bundibugyo ebolavirus (BDBV) outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), as it potentially became the largest Ebola outbreak on record with 6,686 confirmed cases and 3,226 deaths reported as of 6 September 2026.
Design and population
A susceptible-exposed-infectious-deceased-recovered (SEIDR) model was developed to evaluate early transmission dynamics, the current epidemic trajectory, and cross-border spillover risk, incorporating three categories of interventions: public self-protection, safe burial, and treatment and convalescence.
Core methods
The study used International Migrant Stock data and Facebook IP-based mobility data with the Prophet forecasting model to assess spillover risk, and integrated the non-dominated sorting genetic algorithm II (NSGA-II) with Pontryagin's minimum principle (PMP) to derive a time-varying optimal control strategy.
Main results
As of 31 July 2026, the basic reproduction number (\$\:{\mathcal{R}}_{0}\$) was 1.83 (95% CI: 1.81–1.84). Sensitivity analyses identified 28 March as the optimal start date of sustained transmission, with 31 March to 3 April as the most likely onset window.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that when 58.12% of the susceptible population adopted protective behaviours, the transmission chain could be effectively interrupted, highlighting the potential impact of community-level interventions.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the development of targeted control strategies to mitigate the spread of the Bundibugyo ebolavirus and reduce global importation risk, particularly in high-risk countries.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relies on cumulative confirmed case data up to 31 July 2026, which may not capture the full extent of the outbreak. The evidence strength is moderate due to the reliance on model-based projections and assumptions about population behaviour.

心理冲击能力:非洲埃博拉防备的一个重要但被忽视的支柱

Psychological surge capacity: an essential but overlooked pillar of Ebola preparedness in Africa
作者： Minh Cường Dương, Herry Susanto, Thuỳ Anh Bùi, Tri Nugraha Susilawati, Minh Cuong Duong, Thuy Anh Bui
摘要中文翻译中部非洲正在发生的埃博拉疫情 暴发 主要影响刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达,代表着一场迅速演变的公共卫生危机。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
中部非洲正在发生的埃博拉疫情 暴发 主要影响刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达,代表着一场迅速演变的公共卫生危机。
设计与对象
该研究是一篇社论评论,反映了非洲埃博拉病毒防范的心理方面,重点关注刚果民主共和国和乌干达。
核心方法
这些方法涉及对现有文献和公共卫生报告的审查,以强调心理冲击能力是埃博拉病毒防范中一个关键但尚未得到充分重视的组成部分。
主要结果
社论指出,缺乏对心理准备的关注是当前埃博拉应对策略的一个重大缺陷。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为强调需要将心理准备纳入更广泛的埃博拉 暴发 公共卫生策略中,并强调这是该领域的一个新颖视角。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,解决心理冲击能力可以提高非洲公共卫生应对埃博拉 暴发 的有效性。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于编辑审查,不提供原始数据,因此证据强度中等。

Psychological surge capacity: an essential but overlooked pillar of Ebola preparedness in Africa

Authors: Minh Cường Dương, Herry Susanto, Thuỳ Anh Bùi, Tri Nugraha Susilawati, Minh Cuong Duong, Thuy Anh Bui
Original abstract (English)
The ongoing Ebola disease outbreak in Central Africa, primarily affecting the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda, represents a rapidly evolving public health crisis.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The ongoing Ebola disease outbreak in Central Africa, primarily affecting the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda, represents a rapidly evolving public health crisis.
Design and population
The study is an editorial commentary that reflects on the psychological aspects of Ebola preparedness in Africa, focusing on the DRC and Uganda.
Core methods
The methods involve a review of existing literature and public health reports to highlight the psychological surge capacity as a critical but under-addressed component of Ebola preparedness.
Main results
The editorial identifies the lack of attention to psychological preparedness as a significant gap in current Ebola response strategies.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the findings as emphasizing the need to integrate psychological preparedness into broader public health strategies for Ebola outbreaks, highlighting this as a novel perspective in the field.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that addressing psychological surge capacity could enhance the effectiveness of public health responses to Ebola outbreaks in Africa.
Limitations and evidence strength

后新冠时代的传染病威胁:监测 脆弱性、大流行风险和全球准备

Converging infectious disease threats in the post-COVID era: surveillance fragility, pandemic risk, and global preparedness
作者： Abdi Y, Issack Z
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估 COVID-19 后的全球传染病格局,重点关注人畜共患病的出现、抗菌素耐药性和气候敏感疾病扩张等威胁的融合,以及 监测 和治理的结构性失败如何放大这些风险。背景强调,COVID-19 大流行暴露了全球 监测 系统的严重弱点,但容易发生大流行的威胁非但没有消退,反而加剧了。
设计与对象
这篇综合叙述性综述是通过对 PubMed、Scopus 和 Web of Science 进行有针对性的文献检索,以及来自世界卫生组织、非洲疾病预防控制中心和世界银行的灰色文献构建的。检索时间范围侧重于 2020 年 1 月至 2026 年 6 月期间发表的文献,但有意纳入 2010 年至 2019 年的基础研究,以提供必要的流行病学和生态背景。该评论包括提供与新冠疫情后 暴发 基础设施和 暴发 应对的地缘政治决定因素相关的经验数据、流行病学趋势或政策分析的文章。
核心方法
该方法涉及筛选文章标题和摘要的相关性,仅限于英语出版物。进行了手动向后引文检索,以确定进一步的相关来源,并且排除了专门关注没有公共卫生或 监测 背景的临床管理的研究。综合文献按主题组织分析三个领域:暴发 风险的生物驱动因素、监测 系统的结构碎片化以及影响全球卫生治理的地缘政治动态。
主要结果
The review found that as of March 2024, the COVID-19 pandemic had produced approximately 774.8 million confirmed cases and 7 million official deaths globally. It also noted that in 2024, the number of pandemic-prone and epidemic-prone disease outbreaks worldwide was estimated at 301, with approximately 90% associated with known pathogens such as COVID-19, dengue, yellow fever, Oropouche virus disease, and influenza. A June 2024 analysis identified at least 13 infectious diseases showing a resurgence post-pandemic, with over 40 countries or territories reporting at least one resurgence at 10-fold or more over their pre-pandemic baseline.
解释与创新
作者解释说,全球传染病格局正变得更加复杂、不可预测和相互关联,过去 暴发 暴露的失败尚未产生相应的结构性反应。新颖之处在于绘制了新冠疫情后人畜共患 暴发、耐药感染、媒介 和水传播疾病激增的情况,并将这些趋势与分散的实验室网络、不公平的基因组能力以及人类、动物和环境系统的整合薄弱联系起来。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,迫切需要对 One Health 整合、区域基因组平台和基于社区的 监测 进行再分配投资,以加强全球流行病防范。他们还强调了解决脆弱性的地缘政治和社会经济决定因素的重要性,例如与冲突相关的 监测 崩溃和 疫苗 不平等。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到对现有文献的依赖以及人工智能预测和废水 监测 等数字工具可能存在数据偏差的限制。证据是中等的,因为它综合了当前的趋势和政策分析,但不包括原始经验数据或来自特定 暴发 的直接测量。

Converging infectious disease threats in the post-COVID era: surveillance fragility, pandemic risk, and global preparedness

Authors: Abdi Y, Issack Z
Original abstract (English)
This narrative review synthesizes contemporary evidence on three converging drivers of pandemic risk zoonotic emergence, antimicrobial resistance, and climate-sensitive disease expansion and examines how structural failures in surveillance and global health governance amplify their impact. Drawing on recent epidemiological analyses, policy reports, and One Health evaluations, the review maps post-COVID surges in zoonotic outbreaks, drug-resistant infections, and vector and water-borne diseases, and links these trends to fragmented laboratory networks, inequitable genomic capacity, and weak integration across human, animal, and environmental systems. The review argues that without redistributive investment in One Health integration, regional genomic platforms, community-based surveillance, and enforceable IHR-based accountability, the world will continue to detect converging threats late and respond inequitably, leaving pandemic preparedness structurally fragile in the post-COVID era.Keywords: antimicrobial resistance, climate-sensitive diseases, infectious disease surveillance, one health, pandemic preparedness 2.MethodologyTo construct this integrative narrative review, a targeted literature search was conducted to identify peer-reviewed articles, epidemiological reports, and global health policy documents. AbstractThe COVID-19 pandemic exposed profound weaknesses in global infectious disease surveillance yet, in its aftermath, pandemic-prone threats have intensified rather than receded. It further interrogates the geopolitical and socioeconomic determinants of vulnerability, including conflict-related surveillance collapse, vaccine inequity, and the political economy of pandemic financing. Emerging digital tools from AI-enabled forecasting to wastewater and social me...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate the global infectious disease landscape post-COVID-19, focusing on the convergence of threats such as zoonotic emergence, antimicrobial resistance, and climate-sensitive disease expansion, and how structural failures in surveillance and governance amplify these risks. The background highlights that the COVID-19 pandemic revealed critical weaknesses in global surveillance systems, yet pandemic-prone threats have intensified rather than receded.
Design and population
This integrative narrative review was constructed using a targeted literature search across PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science, as well as grey literature from WHO, Africa CDC, and the World Bank. The search timeframe focused on literature published between January 2020 and June 2026, but foundational studies from 2010–2019 were intentionally included to provide necessary epidemiological and ecological context. The review included articles that provided empirical data, epidemiological trends, or policy analyses related to post-COVID surveillance infrastructure and geopolitical determinants of outbreak response.
Core methods
The methodology involved screening article titles and abstracts for relevance, restricted to English-language publications. A manual backward citation search was performed to identify further relevant sources, and studies focusing exclusively on clinical management without a public health or surveillance context were excluded. The synthesized literature was thematically organized to analyze three domains: biological drivers of outbreak risk, structural fragmentation of surveillance systems, and geopolitical dynamics influencing global health governance.
Main results
The review found that as of March 2024, the COVID-19 pandemic had produced approximately 774.8 million confirmed cases and 7 million official deaths globally. It also noted that in 2024, the number of pandemic-prone and epidemic-prone disease outbreaks worldwide was estimated at 301, with approximately 90% associated with known pathogens such as COVID-19, dengue, yellow fever, Oropouche virus disease, and influenza. A June 2024 analysis identified at least 13 infectious diseases showing a resurgence post-pandemic, with over 40 countries or territories reporting at least one resurgence at 10-fold or more over their pre-pandemic baseline.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that the global infectious disease landscape is becoming more complex, unpredictable, and interconnected, with the failures exposed by past outbreaks not yet producing a commensurate structural response. The novelty lies in mapping post-COVID surges in zoonotic outbreaks, drug-resistant infections, and vector and water-borne diseases, and linking these trends to fragmented laboratory networks, inequitable genomic capacity, and weak integration across human, animal, and environmental systems.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the urgent need for redistributive investment in One Health integration, regional genomic platforms, and community-based surveillance to strengthen global pandemic preparedness. They also highlight the importance of addressing geopolitical and socioeconomic determinants of vulnerability, such as conflict-related surveillance collapse and vaccine inequity.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on existing literature and the potential for data bias in digital tools like AI-enabled forecasting and wastewater surveillance. The evidence is moderate, as it synthesizes current trends and policy analyses but does not include original empirical data or direct measurements from specific outbreaks.

死亡率 监测 在乌干达 2025 年苏丹病毒病 暴发 病例检测和响应中的作用。

The Role of Mortality Surveillance in Case Detection and Response During the 2025 Sudan Virus Disease Outbreak in Uganda.
作者： Rubangakene Moses, Caroline Kyozira, Douglas Bulafu, Mgamb Elizabeth, Rawlance Ndejjo, Okware Solome, Abok Patrick, Agwang Winnie, Steven N. Kabwama, Katushabe Edson
摘要中文翻译背景:乌干达等发展中国家面临着遏制苏丹病毒病(SVD)等丝状病毒 暴发 的挑战。乌干达 2025 年苏丹病毒病 暴发 主要影响城市和城郊地区。为了减少 暴发,死亡率 监测 被纳入响应策略中。本文介绍了 死亡率 监测 的实施方式、其对病例检测和响应行动触发的贡献,以及 暴发 期间实施的促进因素和障碍。方法:进行了回顾性描述性文件审查,分析了在 暴发 声明(2025 年 1 月 30 日)和正式结束(2025 年 4 月 26 日)期间事件管理团队生成的情况报告、会议记录、技术简报和 死亡率 监测 摘要。我们描述了实施流程、死亡率 监测 对病例检测和响应的贡献,以及 2025 年苏丹病毒病 (SVD) 暴发 期间实施的促进因素和障碍。结果:在2025年苏丹病毒病暴发期间,死亡率 监测被迅速激活并整合到活跃的监测中。死亡率 监测 是系统地进行的,涉及以下社区参与;死亡通知;流行病学调查;以及遏制措施,例如接触者追踪、隔离以及安全和有尊严的埋葬。死亡率 监测 有助于病例发现:暴发 期间登记的病例中有 3/14(21.4%)是通过死亡警报触发的调查发现的。这些检测为公共卫生措施提供了信息,例如接触者追踪和接触者隔离,这可能有助于中断潜在的 传播 链。主要促进因素包括强有力的协调和领导、数字化数据工具的使用、能力建设以及社区参与和参与。持续存在的障碍包括非正式住区和私人诊所的漏报、样本运输的后勤延误以及 暴发 期间缺乏 死亡率 监测 指南。结论:乌干达的 2025 年苏丹病毒病 暴发 证明了将 死亡率 监测 纳入 暴发 检测和响应的潜在价值,特别是在城市和非正式环境中。加强 死亡率 监测 系统应被视为面临反复丝状病毒威胁的低收入和中等收入国家流行病防范和应对战略的核心组成部分。

范围与问题
乌干达 2025 年苏丹病毒病 暴发 主要影响城市和城郊地区。
证据基础与方法
进行了回顾性描述性文件审查,分析了在 暴发 声明(2025 年 1 月 30 日)和正式结束(2025 年 4 月 26 日)期间事件管理团队生成的情况报告、会议纪要、技术简报和 死亡率 监测 摘要。
共识与结论
在2025年苏丹病毒病暴发期间,死亡率 监测被迅速激活并整合到活跃的监测中。
争议与缺口
持续存在的障碍包括非正式住区和私人诊所的漏报、样本运输的后勤延误以及 暴发 期间缺乏 死亡率 监测 指南。
科研与实践启示
乌干达的 2025 年苏丹病毒病 暴发 证明了将 死亡率 监测 纳入 暴发 检测和响应的潜在价值,特别是在城市和非正式环境中。

The Role of Mortality Surveillance in Case Detection and Response During the 2025 Sudan Virus Disease Outbreak in Uganda.

Authors: Rubangakene Moses, Caroline Kyozira, Douglas Bulafu, Mgamb Elizabeth, Rawlance Ndejjo, Okware Solome, Abok Patrick, Agwang Winnie, Steven N. Kabwama, Katushabe Edson
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Developing countries like Uganda, face challenges in containing filovirus outbreaks such as Sudan virus disease (SVD). The 2025 Sudan Virus Disease outbreak in Uganda predominantly affected urban and peri-urban areas. To curtail the outbreak, mortality surveillance was integrated into the response strategies. This paper describes how mortality surveillance was implemented, its contribution to case detection and to the triggering of response actions, and the facilitators and barriers to its implementation during this outbreak. METHODS: A retrospective descriptive document review was conducted, analyzing situation reports, meeting minutes, technical briefs, and mortality surveillance summaries generated by the Incident Management Team between the declaration of the outbreak (30 January 2025) and its official end (26 April 2025). We described the implementation processes, the contribution of mortality surveillance to case detection and response, and the facilitators and barriers to its implementation during the 2025 Sudan virus disease (SVD) outbreak. RESULTS: During the 2025 Sudan Virus Disease outbreak, mortality surveillance was rapidly activated and integrated into active surveillance. Mortality surveillance was conducted systematically involving the following community engagement; death notification; epidemiological investigations; and containment measures such as contact tracing, quarantine, and safe and dignified burials. Mortality surveillance contributed to case detection: 3/14 (21.4%) cases registered during the outbreak were identified through investigations triggered by death alerts. These detections informed public health measures such as contact tracing and quarantine of contacts, which may have contributed to interrupting potential transmission chains. Key facilitators included strong coordination and leadership, the use of digitized data tools, capacity building and community engagement and involvement. Persistent barriers included underreporting from informal settlements and private clinics, logistical delays in sample transportation, and lack of mortality surveillance guidelines during the outbreak. CONCLUSION: The 2025 Sudan virus disease outbreak in Uganda demonstrates the potential value of integrating mortality surveillance into outbreak detection and response, particularly in urban and informal settings. Strengthening mortality surveillance systems should be considered a core component of epidemic preparedness and response strategies in low- and middle-income countries facing recurrent filovirus threats.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The 2025 Sudan Virus Disease outbreak in Uganda predominantly affected urban and peri-urban areas.
Evidence base and method
A retrospective descriptive document review was conducted, analyzing situation reports, meeting minutes, technical briefs, and mortality surveillance summaries generated by the Incident Management Team between the declaration of the outbreak (30 January 2025) and its official end (26 April 2025).
Consensus and conclusions
During the 2025 Sudan Virus Disease outbreak, mortality surveillance was rapidly activated and integrated into active surveillance.
Controversies and gaps
Persistent barriers included underreporting from informal settlements and private clinics, logistical delays in sample transportation, and lack of mortality surveillance guidelines during the outbreak.
Research and practice implications
The 2025 Sudan virus disease outbreak in Uganda demonstrates the potential value of integrating mortality surveillance into outbreak detection and response, particularly in urban and informal settings.

Early Action Review of Detection, Notification, and Response Timeliness during Cross-Border Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Uganda, 2026

Early Action Review of Detection, Notification, and Response Timeliness during Cross-Border Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Uganda, 2026

Authors: Joshua Kayiwa, Mudarshiru Bbuye, Ritah Atuhaire, Doreen Nyakato, Mathew Tut, Bayad Nozad, Jovan Baryamujura, Bernard Lubwama, Solome Okware, Pamela Kajumba
Original abstract (English)
Volume 32, Number 10—October 2026 Synopsis Early Action Review of Detection, Notification, and Response Timeliness during Cross-Border Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Uganda, 2026 Suggested citation for this article Abstract Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD), an Ebola virus species with no licensed vaccine or therapeutic, reemerged in May 2026 as a cross-border outbreak in Uganda and the Democratic Republic of the Congo. During a 2-day workshop, July 8–9, 2026, we conducted an early action review of the outbreak response using the 7-1-7 framework (7 days to detect, 1 day to notify, 7 days to complete early response actions) to assess timeliness and identify bottlenecks and enablers across 9 response pillars. WHO recommends that an early action review (EAR), an agile, structured methodology for assessing timeliness and identifying bottlenecks and enablers using the 7-1-7 tool, be conducted for all outbreaks of public health significance (22). In this article, we present the process, results, and lessons learned from that review. Study Design and Setting We conducted a retrospective, multipillar programmatic review using WHO’s standardized EAR methodology and 7-1-7 assessment tool (22). Disclaimer: Early release articles are not considered as final versions. Any changes will be reflected in the online version in the month the article is officially released. Uganda declared its outbreak on May 15, 2026; by July 8, the country had recorded 20 confirmed cases (15 imported, 5 locally transmitted) and a case-fatality rate of 15%. Uganda met all 3 targets: detection in 6 days, notification in <1 day, and response completion in 2 days. Scope and question

Consensus and conclusions
Reported CFRs for BVD are generally lower than for Ebola caused by Zaire ebolavirus (6).
Controversies and gaps
On May 15, 2026, both Uganda and the DRC declared separate outbreaks, a transboundary BVD event, from which cases were epidemiologically linked to Ituri Province, DRC (12).
Research and practice implications
By July 8, 2026, Uganda had recorded 20 confirmed cases, of which 15 were directly imported from the DRC, underscoring the cross-border nature of the outbreak and the need for sustained joint surveillance and coordinated screening along the Uganda–DRC corridor.

从 暴发 到区域准备工作的响应:重新构想 mRNA 疫苗 平台,以应对东非的埃博拉病毒

From outbreak response to regional readiness: reimagining mRNA vaccine platforms for Ebola preparedness in East Africa
作者： Ugwu Okechukwu Paul-Chima, Martin Nduwimana
摘要中文翻译埃博拉病仍然是东非持续存在的公共卫生威胁,人畜共患病、跨境流动、脆弱的卫生系统和公众焦虑交织在一起。尽管获得许可的 疫苗 改善了针对扎伊尔埃博拉病毒病的准备工作,但苏丹埃博拉病毒和本迪布焦埃博拉病毒仍存在重大差距,这两种病毒都构成重大区域风险。本观点认为,mRNA 疫苗 平台不仅应被视为紧急响应工具,而且应被视为重要的准备基础设施。它们的价值在于能够快速从病毒测序到抗原设计、小规模生产、免疫学评估和 暴发 部署,减少与传统病原体特异性 疫苗 开发相关的延迟。然而,仅靠技术速度并不能保护医护人员、边境人口或脆弱的农村社区。有效的准备需要与 基因组监测 整合、道德临床试验准备、简化的监管途径、可靠的冷链系统、社区参与以及非洲对 疫苗 制造的有意义的参与。可持续的 mRNA 准备战略还必须解决影响非洲科学创新的结构性挑战,包括对不稳定的捐助资金的依赖、外部支持的减少、供应链的脆弱性和有限的国内投资。我们认为,东非需要一个基于 mRNA 的埃博拉防备生态系统,能够应对苏丹、本迪布焦和扎伊尔埃博拉病毒威胁,同时加强 监测、诊断、感染预防、接触者追踪和安全临床护理。关键的挑战不仅仅是在 暴发 发生时做出反应,而是在下一次流行病出现之前将准备工作制度化。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
有效的准备需要与 基因组监测 整合、道德临床试验准备、简化的监管途径、可靠的冷链系统、社区参与以及非洲对 疫苗 制造的有意义的参与。
证据基础与方法
它们的价值在于能够快速从病毒测序到抗原设计、小规模生产、免疫学评估和 暴发 部署,减少与传统病原体特异性 疫苗 开发相关的延迟。
共识与结论
然而,仅靠技术速度并不能保护医护人员、边境人口或脆弱的农村社区。
争议与缺口
可持续的 mRNA 准备战略还必须解决影响非洲科学创新的结构性挑战,包括对不稳定的捐助资金的依赖、外部支持的减少、供应链的脆弱性和有限的国内投资。
科研与实践启示
我们认为,东非需要一个基于 mRNA 的埃博拉防备生态系统,能够应对苏丹、本迪布焦和扎伊尔埃博拉病毒威胁,同时加强 监测、诊断、感染预防、接触者追踪和安全临床护理。

From outbreak response to regional readiness: reimagining mRNA vaccine platforms for Ebola preparedness in East Africa

Authors: Ugwu Okechukwu Paul-Chima, Martin Nduwimana
Original abstract (English)
Ebola disease remains a persistent public health threat in East Africa, where zoonotic spillover, cross-border mobility, fragile health systems, and public anxiety intersect. Although licensed vaccines have improved preparedness against Zaire ebolavirus disease, major gaps remain for Sudan and Bundibugyo ebolaviruses, both of which pose significant regional risks. This Perspective argues that mRNA vaccine platforms should be viewed not merely as emergency-response tools but as essential preparedness infrastructure. Their value lies in the ability to rapidly progress from viral sequencing to antigen design, small-scale production, immunological evaluation, and outbreak deployment, reducing delays associated with conventional pathogen-specific vaccine development. However, technological speed alone cannot protect healthcare workers, border populations, or vulnerable rural communities. Effective preparedness requires integration with genomic surveillance, ethical clinical trial readiness, streamlined regulatory pathways, reliable cold-chain systems, community engagement, and meaningful African participation in vaccine manufacturing. A sustainable mRNA preparedness strategy must also address structural challenges affecting scientific innovation in Africa, including dependence on unstable donor funding, declining external support, supply-chain vulnerabilities, and limited domestic investment. We contend that East Africa needs an mRNA-enabled Ebola preparedness ecosystem capable of responding to Sudan, Bundibugyo, and Zaire ebolavirus threats while strengthening surveillance, diagnostics, infection prevention, contact tracing, and safe clinical care. The critical challenge is not simply responding to outbreaks when they occur, but institutionalizing readiness before the next epidemic emerges.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Effective preparedness requires integration with genomic surveillance, ethical clinical trial readiness, streamlined regulatory pathways, reliable cold-chain systems, community engagement, and meaningful African participation in vaccine manufacturing.
Evidence base and method
Their value lies in the ability to rapidly progress from viral sequencing to antigen design, small-scale production, immunological evaluation, and outbreak deployment, reducing delays associated with conventional pathogen-specific vaccine development.
Consensus and conclusions
However, technological speed alone cannot protect healthcare workers, border populations, or vulnerable rural communities.
Controversies and gaps
A sustainable mRNA preparedness strategy must also address structural challenges affecting scientific innovation in Africa, including dependence on unstable donor funding, declining external support, supply-chain vulnerabilities, and limited domestic investment.
Research and practice implications
We contend that East Africa needs an mRNA-enabled Ebola preparedness ecosystem capable of responding to Sudan, Bundibugyo, and Zaire ebolavirus threats while strengthening surveillance, diagnostics, infection prevention, contact tracing, and safe clinical care.

塞拉利昂在埃博拉疫情之前、期间和之后的剖腹产率和产科实践:全国家庭研究的结果。

Caesarean section rates and obstetric practices in Sierra Leone before, during, and after the Ebola epidemic: Results from a nationwide household study.
作者： Josien Westendorp, Hanna M Mathéron, Alex J van Duinen, Momoh L Mansaray, Lora Daskalska, Osman Sankoh, Risa Hoffmann-Lonnée, Reinou S Groen, Martin P Grobusch, Håkon A Bolkan
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
BackgroundThrough health system reforms Sierra Leone has achieved reductions from 1,603 to 354 maternal deaths per 100,000 live births between 2000 and 2023, but progress has been uneven and severely disrupted by the 2013-2016 Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak.
证据基础与方法
国家调查跟踪了关键的孕产妇健康指标,但对卫生系统冲击期间和之后的辅助阴道分娩和妇女获得紧急产科护理(包括剖腹产)的了解有限。使用相同的方法将数据与 2012 年家庭调查进行比较,并在国家卫生系统举措和 EVD 暴发 时间表的背景下进行解释。12-50 岁的女性提供了产科病史。
共识与结论
在 914 名怀孕 2,094 次的妇女中,剖腹产率从埃博拉病毒病前的 1.6% 上升至埃博拉病毒病后的 3.7%,而辅助阴道分娩在 暴发 期间降至零,并重新出现在 2.9%。在医院分娩的比例从埃博拉病毒病爆发前的 57.6% 上升至埃博拉疫情爆发后的 73.8%,在家分娩的比例下降至 16.3%。几乎一半的埃博拉病毒病后分娩妇女步行到达设施,救护车的使用仍然很少。在埃博拉病毒病 暴发 后,塞拉利昂的产科服务使用率继续改善,但交通障碍以及辅助阴道分娩和剖腹产率仍然较低。
争议与缺口
十年来,女孩的教育和识字率没有显着改善。
科研与实践启示
加强女童教育、转诊系统、紧急产科能力和技能保留对于有弹性、公平的孕产妇护理仍然至关重要。

Caesarean section rates and obstetric practices in Sierra Leone before, during, and after the Ebola epidemic: Results from a nationwide household study.

Authors: Josien Westendorp, Hanna M Mathéron, Alex J van Duinen, Momoh L Mansaray, Lora Daskalska, Osman Sankoh, Risa Hoffmann-Lonnée, Reinou S Groen, Martin P Grobusch, Håkon A Bolkan
Original abstract (English)
BackgroundThrough health system reforms Sierra Leone has achieved reductions from 1,603 to 354 maternal deaths per 100,000 live births between 2000 and 2023, but progress has been uneven and severely disrupted by the 2013-2016 Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak. National surveys track key maternal health indicators but provide limited insight into assisted vaginal delivery and women's access to emergency obstetric care, including caesarean section, during and after health system shocks.ObjectivesTo describe patterns of caesarean section and assisted vaginal delivery, place and mode of transport for childbirth before, during, and after the EVD outbreak from a community perspective, and assess changes compared with earlier nationwide household study data.DesignCross-sectional household survey.MethodsA district and urban-rural weighted random cluster design selected enumerator areas with probability proportional to population size. Women aged 12-50 years provided obstetric histories. Data were compared with the 2012 household survey using the same methodology and interpreted in the context of national health system initiatives and the EVD outbreak timeline. Descriptive statistics and chi-square tests examined differences across time periods.ResultsAmong 914 women with 2,094 pregnancies, caesarean section rates rose from 1.6% pre-to 3.7% post-EVD, while assisted vaginal delivery dropped to zero during the outbreak and re-emerged at 2.9%. Facility births increased from 57.6% pre-to 73.8% post-EVD, with home deliveries declining to 16.3%. Almost half of women delivering post-EVD reached facilities on foot, and ambulance use remained minimal. No substantial improvement was observed in girls' education and literacy over the decade.ConclusionObstetric service use in Sierra Leone continued to improve after the EVD outbreak, but transport barriers and low assisted vaginal delivery and caesarean section rates persist. Strengthening girls' education, referral systems, emergency obstetric capacity and skills retention remain essential for resilient, equitable maternal care.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
BackgroundThrough health system reforms Sierra Leone has achieved reductions from 1,603 to 354 maternal deaths per 100,000 live births between 2000 and 2023, but progress has been uneven and severely disrupted by the 2013-2016 Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak.
Evidence base and method
National surveys track key maternal health indicators but provide limited insight into assisted vaginal delivery and women's access to emergency obstetric care, including caesarean section, during and after health system shocks. Data were compared with the 2012 household survey using the same methodology and interpreted in the context of national health system initiatives and the EVD outbreak timeline. Women aged 12-50 years provided obstetric histories.
Consensus and conclusions
Among 914 women with 2,094 pregnancies, caesarean section rates rose from 1.6% pre-to 3.7% post-EVD, while assisted vaginal delivery dropped to zero during the outbreak and re-emerged at 2.9%. Facility births increased from 57.6% pre-to 73.8% post-EVD, with home deliveries declining to 16.3%. Almost half of women delivering post-EVD reached facilities on foot, and ambulance use remained minimal. Obstetric service use in Sierra Leone continued to improve after the EVD outbreak, but transport barriers and low assisted vaginal delivery and caesarean section rates persist.
Controversies and gaps
No substantial improvement was observed in girls' education and literacy over the decade.
Research and practice implications
Strengthening girls' education, referral systems, emergency obstetric capacity and skills retention remain essential for resilient, equitable maternal care.

IOM:多国 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 - 第 18 号情况报告(2026 年 9 月 7 日至 13 日) - ReliefWeb

IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 18 (7 to 13 September 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The IOM Situation Report No. 18, dated 18 September 2026, details a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). As of 13 September 2026, the DRC has reported 7,258 confirmed cases and 3,510 confirmed deaths, resulting in a crude case fatality ratio of 48.4 per cent. The outbreak is concentrated in Ituri, North Kivu, Haut-Uélé, Tshopo, and Bas-Uélé provinces, with 54 health zones affected. The report highlights the risk of cross-border spread due to ongoing population movements and porous borders. IOM and partners are enhancing preparedness measures, including surveillance, risk communication, and operational readiness, to address the outbreak's challenges. The situation remains complex, with humanitarian and mobility factors influencing the response.
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至2026年9月13日,暴发至少自2026年9月7日起一直在进行。情况报告于2026年9月18日发布。
地点与对象
暴发 集中在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的伊图里省、北基伍省、上韦莱省、乔波省和下韦莱省。由于跨境人口流动,受影响的人口包括这些省份和邻近国家的个人。
事件
国际移民组织第18号情况报告确认刚果民主共和国存在多国本迪布焦埃博拉病毒病 暴发,截至2026年9月13日,已有7,258例确诊病例,3,510例确诊死亡。该报告由国际移民组织(IOM)和刚果民主共和国卫生部发布。
规模、影响与风险
The outbreak has affected 54 health zones in the DRC. The crude case fatality ratio is 48.4 per cent. The risk of cross-border spread remains elevated due to population movements and porous borders.
应对、状态与不确定性
暴发 继续在复杂的人道主义和流动背景下展开。入境口岸、边境社区和高风险地点的准备能力至关重要。IOM 和合作伙伴正在加强对流动模式的理解,并实施 监测、风险沟通和运营准备措施。

The IOM Situation Report No. 18, dated 18 September 2026, details a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). The outbreak is concentrated in Ituri, North Kivu, Haut-Uélé, Tshopo, and Bas-Uélé provinces, with 54 health zones affected. The report highlights the risk of cross-border spread due to ongoing population movements and porous borders. Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo + 10 more IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 18 (7 to 13 September 2026) Format Situation Report Source IOM Posted 18 Sep 2026 Originally published 18 Sep 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 1.58 MB) SITUATION OVERVIEW According to the Ministry of Health of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), as of 13 September 2026, the country had reported 7,258 confirmed cases of Ebola disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus, including 3,510 confirmed deaths, resulting in a crude case fatality ratio of 48.4 per cent. The outbreak remains concentrated in Ituri, North Kivu, Haut-Uélé, Tshopo and Bas-Uélé provinces, with 54 health zones (administrative health districts) currently affected. The outbreak continues to unfold within a complex humanitarian and mobility context. Population movements associated with trade, livelihoods, displacement, health-seeking behaviours and family links continue within outbreak-affected areas and across international borders, including neighbouring countries that continue to strengthen preparedness measures due to the risk of cross-border transmission. In this context, preparedness capacities at points of entry (PoEs), border communities and other high-risk locations remain essential to support early warning, detection, reporting and response to potential cross-border public health events. Preparedness activities across affected and at-risk countries continue to be informed by findings from mobility mapping, readiness assessments and cross-border risk analyses. Through population mobility mapping (PMM), flow monitoring and border health interventions, IOM and partners continue to strengthen understanding of population mobility patterns, mobility corridors and informal crossing routes that may influence transmission risks. These approaches support evidence-based prioritization of preparedness investments, including disease surveillance, referral systems, risk communication and community engagement (RCCE) and operational readiness measures. Given ongoing transmission in eastern and north-eastern DRC, the risk of cross-border spread remains elevated in neighbouring countries connected through formal and informal mobility corridors. Porous borders, informal crossings and frequent population movements linked to trade, family ties, displacement, insecurity and access to services may facilitate transmission and compound existing humanitarian vulnerabilities. Time

Location and population
The outbreak is concentrated in Ituri, North Kivu, Haut-Uélé, Tshopo, and Bas-Uélé provinces in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). The affected population includes individuals in these provinces and neighboring countries due to cross-border population movements.
Event
The IOM Situation Report No. 18 confirms a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease outbreak in the DRC, with 7,258 confirmed cases and 3,510 confirmed deaths as of 13 September 2026. The report is issued by the International Organization for Migration (IOM) and the Ministry of Health of the DRC.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has affected 54 health zones in the DRC. The crude case fatality ratio is 48.4 per cent. The risk of cross-border spread remains elevated due to population movements and porous borders.
Response, status and uncertainty
The outbreak continues to unfold within a complex humanitarian and mobility context. Preparedness capacities at points of entry, border communities, and high-risk locations are essential. IOM and partners are strengthening understanding of mobility patterns and implementing surveillance, risk communication, and operational readiness measures.

Published: 2026-09-21  |  Publisher: ReliefWeb

非洲科学家表示,埃博拉应对工作取得进展,但 暴发 仍然是非洲大陆的紧急情况 - ReliefWeb

A high priorityPublic-health news

Ebola Response Shows Progress, but Outbreak Remains a Continental Emergency, African Scientists Say - ReliefWeb

News Brief
The Africa CDC Emergency Consultative Group (ECG) has completed an independent scientific review of the Ebola outbreak caused by Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, which was designated a Public Health Emergency of Continental Security (PHECS) on 17 September 2026. The ECG supports a position of cautious optimism, noting declines in cases and deaths in some hotspots, such as Ituri, but also highlighting active transmission and increases in North Kivu. The available data do not confirm that the outbreak has reached its peak or that transmission has been sustainably interrupted. The ECG recommends maintaining the PHECS designation, scaling up response intensity, addressing persistent community deaths, improving contact identification, strengthening community ownership, and restoring essential health services. They caution against interpreting short-term declines as sustained interruption of transmission and emphasize the need for intensified surveillance in affected and re-emerging health zones. The assessment also highlights the importance of continuing vaccine studies and generating effectiveness and safety evidence, while ensuring informed consent about the uncertainty of protection against Bundibugyo virus.
Original source text (English)
Addis Ababa, Ethiopia, 17 September 2026 – Four months after the Ebola outbreak caused by Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo was designated a Public Health Emergency of Continental Security (PHECS), the Africa CDC Emergency Consultative Group (ECG) has completed an independent scientific review and issued recommendations for the next phase of the response. After reviewing the latest epidemiological, operational and scientific evidence, the ECG supports a position of cautious optimism. Declines in cases and deaths in some hotspots, including Ituri, are encouraging, but the situation remains heterogeneous, with increases in some health zones, particularly in North Kivu. The available data do not yet confirm that the outbreak has reached its peak. The Group therefore recommends maintaining the PHECS, scaling up response intensity, consolidating gains, addressing persistent community deaths and gaps in contact identification, strengthening community ownership, and protecting and restoring essential health services. Transmission: Declines in some hotspots, including Ituri, are encouraging, but active transmission and increases in some health zones, particularly in North Kivu, mean the available data do not yet confirm that the outbreak has reached its peak or that transmission has been sustainably interrupted. Declines in some hotspots, including Ituri, are encouraging, but active transmission and increases in some health zones, particularly in North Kivu, mean the available data do not yet confirm that the outbreak has reached its peak or that transmission has been sustainably interrupted. Level of control: Transmission is not yet consistently controlled across all affected areas. Persistent community deaths, gaps in contact identification and disruption of essential health services remain major concerns. Transmission is not yet consistently controlled across all affected areas. Persistent community deaths, gaps in contact identification and disruption of essential health services remain major concerns. Surveillance: Intensified surveillance is required in affected, newly affected and re-emerging health zones, with rapid reinforcement of response capacities when new infections are detected. Intensified surveillance is required in affected, newly affected and re-emerging health zones, with rapid reinforcement of response capacities when new infections are detected. Vaccination: Continue observational and other vaccine studies, strengthen informed consent on the uncertainty of protection against Bundibugyo virus, and continue generating effectiveness and safety evidence under approved protocols. Continue observational and other vaccine studies, strengthen informed consent on the uncertainty of protection against Bundibugyo virus, and continue generating effectiveness and safety evidence under approved protocols. PHECS status: Maintain the PHECS designation and continental-level coordination while monitoring progress against clearly defined criteria for eventual de-escalation. Maintain the PHECS designation and continental-level coordination while monitoring progress against clearly defined criteria for eventual de-escalation. Immediate priorities: Scale up response intensity, especially in North Kivu; urgently reduce community deaths; strengthen contact identification, tracing and data quality; deepen community ownership; and fast-track the restoration of essential health services. The Group assessed both the direction of the epidemic and the strength of the evidence supporting each conclusion. Epidemic trajectory The ECG supports cautious optimism. Improving trends in some health zones, including Ituri, are encouraging, but the pattern is not uniform and transmission remains active in parts of the affected area. The assessment reflects decreases in cases and deaths in some hotspots alongside improvements in laboratory, treatment, community surveillance, contact tracing and other response capacities, while increases in some health zones, particularly in North Kivu, require continued response intensity. The available data do not yet confirm that the outbreak has reached its peak. The Group cautioned against interpreting short-term declines as sustained interruption of transmission. New infections and re-emergence in health zones that had previously shown improvement require continued vigilance until sustained interruption is demonstrated across affected areas. Level of control The ECG concluded that transmission is not yet consistently controlled across all affected areas and that current gains remain vulnerable to reversal if interventions are relaxed too early. The Group highlighted progress in response capacity while identifying persistent concerns: community deaths remain around 60% of reported deaths, gaps remain in the identification and completeness of contact lists, and the outbreak continues to disrupt essential health services. It recommended maintaining and scaling up the response, protecting gains in laboratory and treatment capacity, community surveillance and contact tracing, and avoiding premature relaxation of interventions while transmission remains heterogeneous. Surveillance in affected and re-emerging health zones The ECG called for stronger contact identification and listing, noting that high percentages of contacts traced can mask gaps in the initial identification and completeness of contact lists. The Group recommended prioritising the quality, completeness and timeliness of data reporting, while maintaining intensified surveillance in newly affected and re-emerging health zones and rapidly reinforcing response capacities when new infections are detected. Vaccination and medical countermeasures Following its review of the available scientific evidence, the ECG recommended continuing observational and other vaccine studies and continuing to generate effectiveness and safety evidence on vaccines used or evaluated in the response. The Group advised that informed consent should clearly communicate that the level of protection against Bundibugyo virus remains under investigation, particularly for vaccines not specifically licensed against Bundibugyo virus disease. Additional vaccines may be evaluated in accordance with approved study protocols. Recommendations for the next phase of the response The ECG recommended that Africa CDC, the Government of the Democratic Republic of the Congo and response partners prioritise: 1. Maintain the PHECS status. Maintain the PHECS designation and continental-level response and coordination while monitoring progress against clearly defined criteria for eventual de-escalation. 2. Scale up response intensity and consolidate gains. Continue current efforts and reinforce response intensity, especially in North Kivu; protect strengthened response capacities and avoid premature relaxation of interventions while transmission remains heterogeneous. 3. Urgently address persistent community deaths. Intensify community engagement, early detection, referral, timely access to treatment and trust-building interventions to address the roughly 60% of reported deaths occurring in communities. 4. Strengthen community ownership and accountability. Move from awareness activities toward meaningful community participation in setting priorities and shaping local interventions, strengthening trust in surveillance, treatment and vaccination. 5. Strengthen contact identification, tracing and surveillance. Improve the completeness of contact identification and listing, data quality and timeliness, and sustain intensified surveillance in affected and re-emerging health zones. 6. Protect and restore essential health services. Fast-track free essential health services to restore immunisation, maternal and child health, antenatal care, nutrition and other primary health care services disrupted by the outbreak, and reduce secondary morbidity and mortality. 7. Continue generating evidence on vaccines. Continue observational and other studies, ensure clear informed consent on uncertainty around protection against Bundibugyo virus, and generate effectiveness and safety evidence under approved protocols. “The evidence gives us grounds for cautious optimism, but it does not yet show consistent control across all affected areas. The response must remain at full intensity, with urgent attention to community deaths, complete contact identification and rapid action wherever transmission re-emerges,” said Prof. Salim Abdool Karim, Chair of the Africa CDC Emergency Consultative Group. “Sustained interruption of transmission has to be demonstrated before de-escalation is considered.” H.E. Dr Jean Kaseya, Director General of Africa CDC, said Africa CDC would act on the ECG’s recommendations. “The recommendation is clear: maintain the PHECS, consolidate the gains and close the gaps that continue to cost lives,” said Dr Kaseya. “Africa CDC will work with the Government of the Democratic Republic of the Congo, communities and partners to intensify the response, strengthen surveillance and contact tracing, restore essential health services and continue building the evidence needed to guide vaccination.” The ECG is chaired by Prof. Salim Abdool Karim and comprises twenty senior African scientists and public health experts: Member Member Prof. Salim Abdool Karim, Chair Prof. Helen Rees Dr David Parirenyatwa Prof. Lucille Blumberg Prof. Jean-Jacques Muyembe Prof. Francine Ntoumi Prof. Rose Leke Fonbang Prof. Jean Nachega Dr Amadou Sall Prof. Oyewale Tomori Prof. Samba Sow Prof. Dimie Ogoina Prof. Abderahmane Maroufi Prof. Maha El Rabbat Prof. Samia Menif Marrakchi Prof. Fawzi Derrar Dr Sultani Matendechero Prof. Nelson Sewankambo Prof. Claude Mambo Muvunyi Prof. Agnes Binagwaho Africa CDC continues to work with the Government of the Democratic Republic of the Congo, affected communities, WHO and response partners to implement the ECG’s recommendations and adapt operations as the epidemiological picture evolves. About the Emergency Consultative Group The Africa CDC Emergency Consultative Group is an independent body of senior African scientists and public health experts. It advises the Director General of Africa CDC during major public health emergencies. Its scientific advice has informed Africa CDC decisions throughout the Ebola response. About Africa CDC Africa CDC is the public health agency of the African Union. As an autonomous institution, Africa CDC supports AU Member States in strengthening health systems, improving disease surveillance, and enhancing emergency preparedness and response. For more information, visit: http://www.africacdc.org and follow Africa CDC on LinkedIn, X, Facebook, and YouTube. Media enquiries: Directorate of Communications and Public Information, Africa CDC | Email: communications@africacdc.org

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Creative Diagnostics 扩展试剂解决方案以支持 暴发 应对 Bundibugyo 病毒 - Menafn

Creative Diagnostics Expands Reagent Solutions To Support Outbreak Response Against Bundibugyo Virus - Menafn
新闻简报Creative Diagnostics has expanded its reagent solutions to support outbreak response against the Bundibugyo virus (BDBV), as reported in a news article published on 2026-09-22. The company's offerings include antibodies, ELISA kits, RT-PCR kits, and nucleic acid standard references, aimed at accelerating viral detection, characterization, and countermeasure development. This response follows a highly fatal BDBV outbreak in Mongbwalu Health Zone, Ituri Province, Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), reported by the World Health Organization (WHO) in May 2026, which resulted in deaths among healthcare workers. BDBV is considered a less virulent Ebola virus with a case fatality rate of 25% to 51%, compared to the almost always fatal rate in non-human primates for the Ebola virus. While no approved vaccines or treatments exist for BDBV, early supportive care can save lives. The outbreak has drawn global attention to the need for effective diagnostic and preventive products against this emerging pathogen.

事件
Creative Diagnostics 扩展了其试剂解决方案,以支持针对 Bundibugyo 病毒 (BDBV) 的 暴发 反应。采取这一行动是为了回应刚果民主共和国发生的 暴发 事件,该事件导致医护人员死亡。该事件的确认是根据该公司的公告和世界卫生组织的报告。
规模、影响与风险
刚果民主共和国的本迪布焦病毒 暴发 具有高度致命性,人类和动物 暴发 的病死率为 25% 至 51%。目前尚无批准用于 BDBV 的 疫苗 或特定 治疗,尽管早期支持护理可以挽救生命。暴发 强调了对诊断和预防工具的迫切需求。
应对、状态与不确定性
Creative Diagnostics 正在积极监测 暴发 并推出了支持 BDBV 感染和 疫苗 研究的产品。世界卫生组织收到了 暴发 的报告,但没有提供有关规模或分辨率的进一步细节。证据基于完整的原始报告,不包括联合限制。

Creative Diagnostics Expands Reagent Solutions To Support Outbreak Response Against Bundibugyo Virus - Menafn

News Brief
Creative Diagnostics has expanded its reagent solutions to support outbreak response against the Bundibugyo virus (BDBV), as reported in a news article published on 2026-09-22. The company's offerings include antibodies, ELISA kits, RT-PCR kits, and nucleic acid standard references, aimed at accelerating viral detection, characterization, and countermeasure development.
